These waits no longer go through atomic_t's wait callback, which only counted wait time and failed attempts for the thread-exit log line by the CPU's timestamp counter, so those waits are no longer counted there.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits' named-thread waits (a frame waiter joined at a close, raw word waiters) run through it, and the full build and suites pass. A mutant whose list leaves out the thread's own sync word fails it: a thread told to end is not woken and its join hangs, and the check after it fails. A mutant whose untimed wait waits once instead of until a word changes passed: spurious wakes are rare and the callers look again. A check fails if a setter takes an atomic_wait::list again.


- **The SPU LLVM recompiler's accurate-RSX PUTLLC16 off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The 16-byte store** of `spu_putllc16_rsx_res` (used with accurate RSX reservation access) compared and swapped guest memory through an `atomic_t<nse_t<v128>>` view. It now takes the plain `nse_t<v128>` pointer and swaps with `vm::guest_cas`, which is `retro_atomic_cas_128` for 16 bytes: the same compare-and-swap of all 16 bytes at once.
  - **Tests:** none of its own: the path needs an SPU thread and the RSX reservation lock. vm::guest_cas's 16-byte form is tested in test_emu_waits (two threads adding to both halves). The full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to the recompiler.


- **A savestate's HLE locks on retro_atomic, off atomic_t; a failed finalize wakes its waiters.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The word** (`hle_locks_t::lock_val`: the HLE calls in it while 0 or more, a finalize under evaluation at -1, finalized at -2) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`. `try_lock` takes it with a compare-and-swap while it is not negative, sleeps on it with `retro_waitaddr_wait` while a finalize is evaluated, and is refused once it is finalized. `unlock` is a fetch-and-subtract, and `try_finalize` moves it with compare-and-swap as before.
    - **A finalize whose second test fails** set the word back to 0 and woke no one, so a caller that came during the evaluation slept on until something else changed the word. It now wakes them with `retro_waitaddr_notify_all`, as the finalize that succeeds did.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits has a caller come during a finalize's evaluation and checks that it sleeps. When the evaluation fails, the caller wakes and gets the lock; when it succeeds, the caller wakes and is refused. Dropping either notify fails it, the one on the failed path included, which is how the code was before. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to the HLE locks.


- **The JIT's disk-space budget on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The budget** (`jit_compiler::m_disk_space`, a quarter of the cache drive's free space at start) is a `retro_atomic_64_t`. Space given back is added with `retro_atomic_fetch_add_64`, as atomic_t's fetch_add did. Space taken is taken off only if that much is left, checked and changed in one compare-and-swap loop, as the fetch_op did.
    - **The macOS JIT write guard's fences** are `retro_atomic_thread_fence_seq_cst` (the acq_rel one at the start is made seq_cst, which is no weaker). JIT.h includes retro_atomic.h in place of util/atomic.hpp.
  - **Tests:** none. A jit_compiler needs LLVM set up and the cache drive; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t, its fences or util/atomic.hpp come back to the JIT.


- **Utilities/cond.h's cond_variable removed (an atomic_t user with no callers).** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **`cond_variable`** (a waiter count in an `atomic_t<u32>`, its waits timed) was used nowhere: sys_rsxaudio.h included its header and used nothing from it. Utilities/cond.h and cond.cpp are deleted, with their lines in Makefile.common and rpcs3/Emu/CMakeLists.txt and the include.
  - **Tests:** none; nothing used it. The full build and suites pass. A check fails if the files or cond_variable come back.


- **stereo_config's live preview removed (its atomic_t flag with it).** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The live preview** (`s_live_preview_enabled`, an `atomic_t<bool>`, and the `m_live_matrices` it chose) is standalone RPCS3's settings dialog's: nothing in the core set either, so `matrices()` never took that branch. The flag, the matrices, their setter and the branch are gone, and with them the only use of `m_in_emulation`, which goes too (the constructor keeps its argument for its callers). The header drops util/atomic.hpp and an unused `<mutex>`.
  - **Tests:** none; the removed branch was never taken. The full build and suites pass. A check fails if the live preview or atomic_t comes back to stereo_config.


- **The IPC manager's made-count on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The count** (`ipc_manager::m_made`, bumped as an object made for a key is published) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`. A caller that finds its key being made by another reads it first, then sleeps on it with `retro_waitaddr_wait`, untimed as before. The maker bumps it with a fetch-and-add and wakes them all with `retro_waitaddr_notify_all`. IPC.h no longer includes util/atomic.hpp.
  - **Tests:** test_held_queues' shared-objects test has four threads ask for the same 64 keys while each is slow to make, so the others wait on the count: every key is made once and all see the same object. With the notify dropped, the waiters never wake and the test hangs until it is killed (timeout after two minutes), rather than failing. A check fails if atomic_t or util/atomic.hpp comes back to IPC.h.


- **cellGcmSys's reserved IO size on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The size** (`gcm_config::reserved_size`, the main memory cellGcmReserveIoMapSize keeps back) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`. `reserve(size, limit)` and `unreserve(size)` check and change it in one compare-and-swap loop each, as atomic_t's fetch_op did: a reserve that would pass the main memory size is refused, and so is giving back more than is kept. `reserved_now()` reads it for cellGcmGetMaxIoMapSize and the unmap walk.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits has two threads reserve 1 MB at a time, eight tries each, against a 5 MB limit: five succeed between them and the total stays at 5 MB. Then it gives back within and beyond what is kept. Mutants that drop either bound check fail it. A mutant reserve that stores instead of compare-and-swapping passed: the two threads seldom meet between the read and the write. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellGcmSys.


- **sceNpTus's unused manager removed (its atomic_t flag with it).** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **`sce_np_tus_manager`** and its title and transaction context structs were declared in sceNpTus.h and used nowhere: nothing made one, nothing read its `atomic_t<bool> is_initialized`, and its methods were defined nowhere. They are deleted, with their `std::map`s and the header's `<map>` include.
  - **Tests:** none; nothing used it. The full build and suites pass. A check fails if the manager or atomic_t comes back to sceNpTus.


- **cellImeJp's initialized flag off atomic_t: the HLE thread's own.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The flag** (`ime_jp_manager::is_initialized`) is a plain bool. Every cellImeJp call that reads or writes it already runs on the HLE thread (`hle_call`), as the rest of the manager does, so it has one thread and needs no atomic. The manager's copy is deleted, as the object map asks.
  - **Tests:** none. The flag is one thread's, and the calls need the HLE thread, which the tests do not run; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellImeJp.


- **cellFont on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **A face's owner** (`cell_font_face::owner`, the CellFont's address, 0 when free) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`: claimed with a compare-and-swap from 0, read with acquire, freed with a release store.
    - **The lent scratch flags** were eight `atomic_t<u32>`. They are now eight bits of one `retro_atomic_int_t`: a scratch is lent with a fetch-or of its bit (lent if the bit was clear) and given back with a fetch-and.
  - **Tests:** none. The table is local to cellFont.cpp and the calls need guest memory; test_font covers the rasterizer, not the table. The full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellFont.


- **The small TLS areas' map: a bit per area in retro_atomic words, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The map** (`s_tls_map` in sys_ppu_thread_.cpp, one entry per small TLS area, up to about 5,400 of them) was an array of `atomic_t<bool>`, scanned one by one with an exchange each. It is now a bit per area in `retro_atomic_int_t` words: `tls_map_take` finds the lowest clear bit of a word and sets it with a fetch-or, retrying if another thread set it first, so it looks at 32 areas per load. `tls_map_give` clears the bit with a fetch-and and says whether it was set, for the double-free error as before. The map shrinks to an eighth.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits has four threads take all 40 areas of a two-word map (the second word partly used): each is taken once, the rest are refused, the last word's spare bits stay clear, and an area given back is taken again while one given twice is refused. A mutant that lets the last word's spare bits be taken fails it, and so does one whose give always says it was taken. A mutant take that does not check the bit was still clear passed: the four threads seldom meet on one word at once. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to the map.


- **cellSearch's state on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The state** (`search_info::state`: not initialized, idle, in progress, initializing, canceling, finalizing) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`. `cas_state` takes it from one state to the next by compare-and-swap and returns the state it found, as atomic_t's compare_and_swap did, so the calls' error codes are unchanged. `set_state` stores and `state_now` reads.
  - **Tests:** none. The state is local to cellSearch.cpp and the calls need guest memory; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellSearch.

The end of a song and the state's end set it and wake the state thread with a retro_waitaddr notify, through `wake_up_thread()`. The state thread sleeps on it with `thread_ctrl::wait_on_word`, untimed as before, then takes it back to 0 and plays the next track.
  - **Tests:** none. The state needs a music handler and its thread; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellMusic.

Same steps as before.

- **cellResc on retro_atomic, off atomic_t; one cellRescInit fills the config.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The initialized flag** (`cell_resc_manager::is_initialized`) is a `retro_atomic_int_t` with three states: 0, `starting` and 1. cellRescInit claims it with a compare-and-swap from 0 to `starting`, fills the config, then stores 1 with release. With atomic_t's check-then-store, two Inits at once could both write the config, and a call could see the flag set before the config was whole.
    - **The other calls** look through `initialized()`, which is true only at 1, read with acquire, so the config is seen whole. cellRescExit ends it with a compare-and-swap from 1 to 0, and leaves an Init under way be.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits has two threads claim at once (one gets it), checks the flag reads uninitialized until the config is published, that an exit during the fill does nothing, and that it can be claimed again after an exit. Three mutants fail it: a claim that stores, an exit that stores 0, and `initialized()` true while starting. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellResc.


- **cellMic's initialized flag off atomic_t: the microphone thread's own.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The flag** (`mic_context::init`) is a plain bool. Every call that reads or writes it already runs on the microphone thread (`mic_call`), as the devices do, so it has one thread and needs no atomic.
  - **Tests:** none. The flag is one thread's, and the calls need the microphone thread, which the tests do not run; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellMic.


- **cellNetCtl on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **cellNetCtlGetInfo's once-logged codes** were 256 `atomic_t<bool>`, one exchange each. They are now a bit per code in eight `retro_atomic_int_t` words: a fetch-or of the code's bit sets it and says whether it was set already. The table goes from 1 KB to 32 bytes.
  - **Tests:** none. The path needs guest memory for the info pointer, which the tests do not map; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellNetCtl.


- **cellCrossController on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The status** (`cross_controller::status`) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`: stored with release on the PPU thread at Initialize and by the connection thread as it ends, read with acquire by the next Initialize.
  - **Tests:** none. The status is set by Initialize's dialog and connection thread, which need the emulator; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellCrossController.


- **cellPhotoExport on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The export's progress** (`photo_export::progress`, 0 to 0xFFFF) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`, stored with release by the export's sysutil callback and read with acquire by cellPhotoExportProgress's, which may run on another PPU thread.
  - **Tests:** none. Both sides run in sysutil callbacks on a PPU thread, which the tests do not run; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellPhotoExport.


- **cellVoice's initialized flag off atomic_t: the HLE thread's own.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The flag** (`voice_manager::is_init`) is a plain bool. Every call that touched it already ran on the HLE thread (`hle_call`) except cellVoiceDebugTopology, which read it from the PPU thread; that call now looks in `hle_call` too. So the flag has one thread and needs no atomic.
  - **Tests:** none. The flag is one thread's now, and the one call moved needs the HLE thread, which the tests do not run; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellVoice.


- **cellSsl on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The initialized flag** (`ssl_manager::is_init`) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`, read through `initialized()`. cellSslInit takes it with a compare-and-swap from 0 to 1 and cellSslEnd ends it with one from 1 to 0, so of two at once one succeeds and the other gets ALREADY_INITIALIZED or NOT_INITIALIZED. atomic_t's check-then-store let both succeed.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits runs two Inits at once, then two Ends at once, twice over: one of each succeeds and the flag follows. A mutant Init or End that stores without the compare-and-swap fails it. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellSsl.


- **sceNpSns on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The initialized flag** (`sce_np_sns_manager::is_initialized`) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`, read through `initialized()`. sceNpSnsFbInit takes it with a compare-and-swap from 0 to 1 and sceNpSnsFbTerm ends it with one from 1 to 0, so of two at once one succeeds and the other gets ALREADY_INITIALIZED or NOT_INITIALIZED. atomic_t's check-then-store let both succeed.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits runs two Inits at once, then two Terms at once, twice over: one of each succeeds and the flag follows. A mutant Term that stores 0 without the compare-and-swap fails it every run. A mutant Init that stores 1 without it passed three runs out of three: the two threads almost never meet in the window between the check and the store. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to sceNpSns.


- **cellPad on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **Whether cellPad is initialized** (`pad_info::max_connect`, with `starting` while a cellPadInit is under way) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`, read once per call through `max_connect_now`/`initialized`, set with `set_max_connect` and taken by cellPadInit and cellPadEnd with `cas_max_connect`, a compare-and-swap as before.
    - **cellPadSetActDirect's log budgets** (the first calls of a session, then the notable ones) are retro_atomic counters counted down with fetch-and-subtract, as the post-decrement did.
  - **Tests:** test_pad_guest has two threads take an uninitialized cellPad at once: one gets it, the other is refused, and a later take from 0 fails. A mutant compare-and-swap that always stores fails it. The log budgets are not tested. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellPad.


- **sceNpTrophy on retro_atomic, off atomic_t; libretro_wait_on for retro_atomic words only.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **A context registration's status callbacks:** the count of those queued for the game is a `retro_atomic_int_t`. The callback the game takes counts it down, and the last one wakes the registration with a retro_waitaddr notify. The registration's wait (the next status once the game has taken this one, or after a few frames without; none at a stop) is unchanged, now in `sce_np_trophy_wait_taken`, with the count-down in `sce_np_trophy_status_taken`.
    - **The manager's initialized flag** is a plain bool: every call reads and writes it on the HLE thread (`hle_call`), so it needs no atomic. Its copy is deleted, as the object map asks.
    - **libretro_wait_on's atomic_t overload** (System.h) is gone, its last caller moved; the retro_atomic one stays.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits checks the registration's wait on a named thread: it sleeps while two callbacks are queued, stays asleep after the first is taken, and is woken by the second with no frame. A mutant that drops the notify fails it, and so does one that never counts down. The test of libretro_wait_on's word and event wakes now uses a retro_atomic word. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to sceNpTrophy or System.h.


- **cellMsgDialog on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The dialog's state** (`MsgDialogBase::state`, a `MsgDialogState`) is a retro_atomic word. Closing and aborting are a compare-and-swap from Open, then a retro_waitaddr notify for the blocking open asleep on it.
    - **The close timer's guest time** (`wait_until`) is a `retro_atomic_64_t`. Its thread waits on the word's low half, through `thread_ctrl::wait_on_word` and the wait list's `set_raw`, as atomic_t waited on it, and is told with a notify on the same address.
    - **The overlay dialog's blocking-open flag** is a `retro_atomic_int_t`, as the overlays' own flags already are.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits checks a dialog's state word: a blocking open stays asleep while the dialog is open and is woken by its abort's compare-and-swap and notify. A mutant `libretro_wait_on` that waits for the wrong value loops instead and fails it. The dialog's own paths (the overlay, the close timer) need the emulator and are not run; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellMsgDialog.

- **lv2's waiter counts on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The counts:** `lwcond_waiters` and `lwmutex_waiters` (the counts a destroy waits on, with the sign bit as its flag), a PRX's `exports_busy` and an SPU group's `spurs_running` are retro_atomic words. They are counted with adds, ors and compare-and-swap loops where atomic_t ran a lambda, and woken with retro_waitaddr on the same address. The SPU group log state is one too.
    - **Waiting:** `thread_ctrl::wait_on_word(word, old, usec)` waits on such a word through the wait list's `set_raw`, with the thread's own wake-ups, as `wait_on` did for an atomic_t. `sys_prx` waits for the exports to be free with retro_waitaddr.
    - **Unchanged:** the SPURS thread throttle (`spurs_running`) still waits for at most the length of one and a half tasks, measured on the wall clock, and still sleeps 300 µs to let another thread take over. Both are RPCS3's and left as they were.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits's named thread now waits through `wait_on_word`. It checks that the thread is asleep while the word holds (at most two passes in 200000 yields), is woken by a notify, and is woken by its end. A mutant that waits for the wrong old value loops instead and fails it. The counts themselves are inside syscalls that need the emulator; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to these lv2 files.

- **The RSX's writes to the game's memory through vm::guest_* and retro_atomic, off atomic_t views.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **16 bytes at once:** a report (ZCULL and `GET_REPORT`), a notify and the 16 bytes after the flip label are stored with one 128-bit release store through `vm::guest_store`, which now stores 16-byte words that way, or changed with `vm::guest_op`.
    - **Semaphores:** a semaphore release is a `vm::guest_store` of the word, then `vm::write<void>` for the memory breakpoint check, which the atomic_t write went through before.
    - **The semaphore wait** (`nv406e` acquire) reads the word with `vm::guest_load`, and `utils::spin_on_cacheline_once` now takes the word itself (4 or 8 bytes) and reads it with retro_atomic's acquire loads. It still watches the cache line with UMWAIT, MWAITX or WFE where the processor has them.
    - **Unchanged:** that wait still gives up after the driver recovery timeout, measured on the wall clock, as before.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits stores 300000 reports while another thread reads them whole, never seeing halves of two, and wakes a semaphore wait by changing the word from another thread. A mutant that stores the 16 bytes as two 8-byte stores fails it. A check fails if atomic_t views come back to these files.

- **The texture cache's counters on retro_atomic, off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** the cache's update tag and section tag (`retro_atomic_64_t`), the per-frame statistics (flushes, misses, speculations, hard faults, uploads, elided copies, mispredictions), the storage's section counts, the unreleased-object count and the memory in use are retro_atomic words. They are read with acquire loads and counted with retro_atomic's adds and subtracts, where atomic_t's operators were sequentially consistent. The Vulkan and OpenGL caches read the unreleased count the same way.
  - **Tests:** none run the texture cache, which needs a renderer; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to the texture cache headers.

- **SPURS's words through vm::guest_* (guest_atomic.h); atomic_be_t and atomic_le_t are gone.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **cellSpurs.h:** the counts, signals, states, events, flags and masks SPURS shares with the game and its SPU programs (`wklReadyCount`, `wklSignal`, `wklState`, `wklEvent`, `wklEnabled`, `sysSrvMessage`, the handler flags, the exception handler, the event flag's control word, the taskset bitsets, the job guard and job chain words and others) are plain fields laid out as the game has them. They are aligned as atomic_t aligned them, and the sizes the module checks are unchanged.
    - **cellSpurs.cpp and cellSpursSpu.cpp** read them with `vm::guest_load` and change them with `guest_store`, `guest_exchange`, `guest_cas`, `guest_op` and `guest_fetch_op`. That includes inside `vm::light_op`, whose `atomic_op` and `fetch_op` now run on vm::guest_*. Where cellSpursSpu changed a word with a plain read-modify-write (the ready counts, the signals, the service message bits, the trace word), it is a compare-and-swap now. The trace update is one operation on the four bytes, so what it checks is what it just changed, as before.
    - **vm_ptr.h:** the guest templates take 1-byte words (through the aligned 4-byte word around them, like 2-byte ones) and 16-byte words (retro_atomic's 128-bit word). `guest_fetch_op` is added, with atomic_t's meaning: an op answering false stores nothing.
    - **types.hpp:** `atomic_be_t` and `atomic_le_t` are removed; nothing uses them.
    - **Unchanged:** the SPURS waits that spin on the game's words still spin, as SPU programs change those words with their own atomics.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits counts two 1-byte words in one 4-byte word up from a thread each, neither losing the other's (40000 adds each). It checks that a fetch_op answering false stores nothing, and adds to both halves of a 16-byte word from two threads 200000 times each, losing nothing. A mutant that writes the 16-byte word without a compare-and-swap, and one whose fetch_op stores when the op answered false, each fail it. Nothing runs SPURS itself; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_be_t or atomic_le_t come back.

- **The PPU's reservation loads and conditional stores through guest_atomic.h, off atomic_be_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** `lwarx`/`ldarx` load the aligned 64 bits of the reservation through `guest_atomic_load_be64`. `stwcx`/`stdcx` compare, store and compare-and-swap them through guest_atomic.h, and make their write access with a 64-bit add of 0, as atomic_t's `+= 0` did. Where the compare-and-swap fails, the value now there is loaded right after it, where atomic_t's compare-exchange handed back the one it saw; that value only seeds the next attempt's quick check, which compares again.
  - **Tests:** none run the PPU's reservation instructions; a ppu_thread needs the whole emulator. guest_atomic.h's 64-bit operations are covered by `test_guest_atomic`, and the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_be_t comes back to PPUThread.cpp.

- **The RSX control words and the driver info through guest_atomic.h, off atomic_be_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **put, get and ref** (`RsxDmaControl`, and `CellGcmControl`, the game's view of the same words) are plain big-endian words in the game's memory again. The FIFO, the RSX thread, the NV methods, cellGcmSys and sys_rsx read and change them through guest_atomic.h: loads, release stores, exchange where atomic_t's store was relied on to be sequentially consistent (setting the reference), an add for the flip command, and an and for the flag bits of put. The put/get pair is still written as one 64-bit store when a DMA control interrupt moves both.
    - **cellGcmSys** waits for get to leave its command buffer on the raw word through the wait list's `set_raw`, and the FIFO wakes it with retro_waitaddr on the same address. No spin, as before.
    - **The driver info's** flip flags, vblank count, last second-vblank time and handler mask are plain words too: or and loads for the flags, a 64-bit add for the count, `vm::guest_op` where a lambda changed them.
    - **A report** (`CellGcmReportData`, 16 bytes) is written with one 128-bit release store instead of atomic_t's, so a reader never sees the time of one report with the value of another.
    - **guest_atomic.h** gains a 32-bit and and or.
    - **Unchanged:** the PPU's lwarx/stwcx emulation (PPUThread.cpp) still views the reservation's 64 bits as atomic_be_t, left for its own patch.
  - **Tests:** `test_guest_atomic` checks and and or with the old value back, and two threads clearing and setting their own bits of one word 2 million times each, neither losing the other's. A mutant that ors with a load and a store fails it in 3 of 5 runs on this 2-CPU machine (the window between the two is a few instructions). No test runs the RSX itself; the full build and suites pass. A check fails if atomic_be_t comes back to GCM.h or sys_rsx.h.

- **cellSync's words through vm::guest_op (guest_atomic.h), off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The control words** of cellSync's mutex, barrier, reader-writer mutex, queue and lock-free queue are plain structures again, laid out as the game has them. They are aligned as atomic_t aligned them, and the sizes the module checks are unchanged.
    - **vm_ptr.h** gains `vm::guest_load`, `guest_store`, `guest_exchange`, `guest_cas` and `guest_op`. These are function templates that copy a 2-, 4- or 8-byte structure whole through guest_atomic.h's unswapped 32- and 64-bit operations (new there). `guest_op` runs the change on a copy until a compare-and-swap takes it, and writes nothing where nothing changed. A 2-byte word (the lock-free queue's packed fields) is changed through the aligned 4-byte word it lies in, the other half kept, retrying if that half moved.
    - **Smaller:** the module's functions are 21524 bytes instead of 23033 (x86-64, -O3). Most got smaller, the queue's lock-free clear and push completion grew.
    - **Unchanged:** the waits (`cellSyncMutexLock`, the barrier, the blocking queue calls) still spin on the game's words, which SPU programs change with their own atomics and nothing on the host is told about.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits takes 60000 tickets of a cellSync mutex from two threads, every ticket once. It counts two 2-byte words that share one 4-byte word from a thread each, neither losing the other's. It also swaps and compares an 8-byte queue word whole, checking its big-endian bytes. A mutant that writes the 4-byte word around a 2-byte one without a compare-and-swap, and one whose `guest_op` stores without one, each fail it. A check fails if atomic_t comes back to cellSync.h.

- **Lightweight mutexes and sys_mmapper's shared area through guest_atomic.h, off atomic_be_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **sys_lwmutex_t** (the owner and waiter count the game keeps in its own memory) is plain big-endian words again. sysPrxForUser's lwmutex and lwcond functions change them through small helpers that use guest_atomic.h: read, set, swap and compare-and-swap the owner, add to the waiters, and set the pair. Where atomic_t ran a lambda on the pair, setting the owner while moving the waiter count is now one 64-bit compare-and-swap loop. Unlocks and owner sets are release stores where atomic_t's were sequentially consistent.
    - **guest_atomic.h** gains a 64-bit add (a subtraction adds the negation).
    - **sys_mmapper**'s shared memory area address, published once from 0, is read and compare-and-swapped through guest_atomic.h. A failed compare-and-swap reads the address the other caller put there, as it can only go from 0 to an address.
    - **Unchanged:** lwmutex locking still spins briefly (`busy_wait`) before going to the syscall, as the PS3 library does; `lwcond_waiters` on the host side is a separate atomic_t, left for its own patch.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits checks a lightweight mutex's words in memory: big-endian bytes with the owner first, an owner compare-and-swap that reports the owner it found, and 100000 waiters counted from two threads, one of them setting the owner at the same time. It also checks the pair's compare-and-swap. `test_guest_atomic` checks the 64-bit add carrying and taking away. A mutant that sets the owner and waiters with a plain store, and one whose owner compare-and-swap reports the expected owner instead of the one it found, each fail it. A check fails if atomic_be_t or atomic_t come back to the lwmutex words or sys_mmapper.

- **Guest words changed from the host through retro_atomic (Emu/Memory/guest_atomic.h); sys_spinlock first.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **guest_atomic.h** (C89, header only) loads, stores, swaps, compare-and-swaps and adds to 4- and 8-byte words of guest memory through retro_atomic. The value is swapped to the PS3's big-endian order (one bswap) on a little-endian host. It is what the HLE modules will use instead of `atomic_be_t`, which is `atomic_t` on a big-endian value.
    - **sys_spinlock** (`sys_spinlock_initialize`, `_lock`, `_trylock`, `_unlock`) takes the lock as a plain big-endian word and changes it through guest_atomic.h. The unlock is a release store where atomic_t's was sequentially consistent, which is all an unlock needs. The functions are the same size as before (x86-64, -O3).
    - **Unchanged:** `sys_spinlock_lock` still spins until the word is free, as the PS3 library does. The game may release the lock with a plain store of its own, which nothing on the host is told about, so there is no event to wait on.
  - **Tests:** `test_guest_atomic` (C89, and under ThreadSanitizer) checks that guest words hold big-endian bytes, that exchange and compare-and-swap see and return the guest's values for 32 and 64 bits, that adds wrap, and that two threads adding to one word lose nothing. A mutant that returns the swapped-out value unswapped, and one that adds with a load and a store, each fail it. A check fails if atomic_be_t comes back to sys_spinlock.

- **Shared pointers on retro_atomic (util/shared_ptr.hpp), off atomic_t.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The reference count** of `single_ptr`, `shared_ptr` and `atomic_ptr` objects is a `retro_atomic_64_t`, counted with acq_rel adds and subtracts. The destructor pointer next to it is a plain pointer: it is set before the object is shared and called only by whoever drops the last reference, after the count's acq_rel.
    - **atomic_ptr's word** (the pointer with the count of references it lends out) is a `retro_atomic_128_t`, changed with compare-and-swap and exchange as before. The loops start from the two halves read apart instead of a whole 16-byte load; a torn pair only fails the compare-and-swap. Where nothing changes, the decision rests on the pointer half, which is read whole. `observe()` and `operator bool` read the pointer half alone. `load()` is 53 instructions instead of 63 and `observe()` 3 instead of 4 (x86-64, -O3); `store()` is 35 instead of 31.
    - **Waiting:** `atomic_ptr::wait(nullptr)` and the notifies park on the `is_non_null` word with retro_waitaddr. The wait list in atomic.hpp names that word through `wait_word()`. The wait takes no timeout now; no caller passed one.
    - atomic_ptr is explicitly not copyable, as it was when its word was an atomic_t.
  - **Tests:** test_emu_waits loads an atomic_ptr from two threads while a third replaces it 100000 times. Every reader gets a live object, and every object is freed exactly once. It also checks compare-and-swap (failing and succeeding), exchange, push_head, the use counts, and a waiter asleep on the null pointer woken by a store. A mutant that frees without counting the lent references, and one that writes the word without a compare-and-swap, each crash it. A check fails if atomic_t or atomic.hpp come back to shared_ptr.hpp.

- **The Patch Manager's options are C89 (rpcs3/libretro/libretro_patch_options.c), off the STL.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The options:** which of a game's patches are listed (one per description, in the order of the descriptions; "All titles" patches only for the games named for them), their keys, sublabels and value choices, and the core's text patches are built in C89 from the patch engine's arrays. They used to be built in maps of strings and vectors. The strings live in blocks of the module's own until the next game; the definitions are one array.
    - **The switches:** turning on one patch of a group turns the others off, and a patch's value choices show only while it is on, as before. The last value of each switch is a flag on its option instead of a map of strings.
    - **patch_config.yml:** the patches turned on, with the values picked, go to the patch engine's writer as a list of choices. The text patches turned on go to the PPU loader as before.
    - **The frontend's values** are read through a plain function that hands back the frontend's own string, instead of a `std::function` that returned a copy.
    - **Kept:** the keys are the same hashes as before, so choices in .opt files stay with their patches. `libretro_patch_manager.cpp` now only reads the patch files and writes patch_config.yml.
  - **Tests:** `test_patch_options` (C89) checks the definitions for a game with a text patch, a group and a configurable value: their order, keys, sublabels, value labels and defaults. It checks that turning one patch of a group on turns the other off, that value choices show with their patch, and the choices for patch_config.yml with the picked value, for both executables. It also checks that both of a group on in the .opt file writes one, with a warning, that an "All titles" patch is listed only for a game named for it and is configured for all, and that games without patches have none. Mutants that skip the sort, the group switch-off, the "All" key or the group check in the choices each fail it. `test_game_patches` still gives every game tried its options. Listing a game's patches takes 33 ms, about the same as after the patch engine change, as reading patch.yml is most of it. A check fails if maps, sets, vectors or `std::function` come back to libretro_patch_manager.

- **The patch engine is C89 (Utilities/patch_db.c), off the STL.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **Reading:** patch.yml and the files like it are read with libretro-common's `ryaml` straight into flat arrays: executables (found by hash in a table of their own), patches, entries, games, configurable values and their allowed values. The strings point into the file's text, which the database keeps; only what YAML spells another way (escapes, block scalars, notes in pieces) is copied. The messages, the checks and the order of the checks are RPCS3's, the configurable values now read before the games so patch_config.yml's values can be matched to them. A load of an anchor inside another stops after 16 levels instead of recursing without end.
    - **Applying:** which patches are on for the game and in what order (any serial and version, then any serial, then any version, then exactly the game), one patch of a group at most, the segment's range, code caves, and allocations that all happen or are all given back are in C89. Patches of one executable now apply in the order of the file, where before it was the order of a hash map. The values are written byte by byte. What only the emulator can do (guest memory, branches, breakpoints, file mounts) stays in `bin_patch.cpp` behind a table of callbacks; `apply` takes the caller's lambda as a template, not as a `std::function`.
    - **patch_config.yml** is written by `patch_db_write_config` from the options turned on (the Patch Manager category), and read during the load, value by value, instead of into a map of maps for every file.
    - **Removed:** patch import, removal and saving of patch files, which only the Qt patch manager called. A hexadecimal function ID in a `jumpf` entry (`module:0x1234abcd`) is now read; the old parse compared against the wrong position and never accepted one.
    - **CMake** now builds `patch_db.c`, and `yaml_value.c` and `init_word.c`, which earlier patches left out of its list. The patch engine version for fetching patch.yml is read from `patch_db.h`.
    - **Speed and size:** reading the 3 MB patch.yml takes 34 ms instead of 127 ms (best of 10, -O3, this machine), and so does listing a game's patches as options. The patch engine's code is 64 KB instead of 190 KB.
  - **Tests:** `test_patch_db` (C89) reads files and checks the entries' values (signed bytes, floats rounded to 32 bits, text, a loaded anchor at an offset), patch_config.yml's on/off and values (one out of range falls back to the default), and errors: another engine version, a file that does not parse, a bad serial, app version, type, offset or value, a duplicate allowed value. It checks that a higher patch version replaces a patch and a lower one does not. It applies patches to memory and checks the bytes, the order of the four kinds, groups across calls, the segment's range, a code cave filled until it has no NOP left, allocations given back when one fails, and patch_config.yml written and read back. Mutants that apply the most specific patch first, keep the least specific patch of a group, ignore the end of a code cave or skip giving back allocations each fail it. `test_game_patches` reads the core's patch.yml with no error, all 985 executables and 2773 patches, and gives every game tried its options. A check fails if maps, sets, `std::function`, stringstreams or regexes come back to bin_patch.

- **stx::init_mutex's word is C89 (rpcs3/util/init_word.c) on `retro_atomic`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The word:** `init_mutex` guards a resource that is set up, used, and taken down: game data, the keyboard and mouse handlers, the on-screen keyboard and message dialogs, the system cache. Its word holds an initialised bit and a count of holders, the one setting up or taking down among them. It is a `retro_atomic_int_t`.
    - **Its changes are C89** in `init_word.c`, with the same compare-and-swap loops as before: taking the init lock (from nothing, or, forced, from initialised while keeping the users), setting it initialised, cancelling, taking and giving back access.
    - **Waiting:** a waiter sleeps on the word with `retro_waitaddr` and every change wakes it, as before. The callbacks and the lock objects stay C++ in `init_mutex.hpp`.
  - **Tests:** `test_init_word` (C89, and a ThreadSanitizer build) checks the init lock is taken from nothing and refused while held or initialised, that access is granted only while initialised and counted, and that a forced take keeps the users. It checks cancel and set-initialised, and that a thread waiting to take the resource down is woken when the last user leaves. It also runs four threads taking and giving back access 200000 times each, and checks the count ends where it began. It was shown to fail with giving back access adding instead of taking away. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to `init_mutex`.


- **The video encoder's running flag (util/media_utils) is off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** the flag that keeps the recording encoder's thread taking frames (cleared on stop, and on a failure to open or finish the file) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`, read with `running()`. The encoder thread's sleep for the next frame waits on the sink's frame count, which is already a `retro_atomic_int_t`, by its address instead of through an `atomic_t` cast.
  - **Tests:** none at runtime: the encoder runs only while a recording is made, which needs the video and audio encoders set up for a game. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to media_utils.


- **Dynamic imports and shared memory's own mapping (util/dyn_lib.hpp, util/vm.hpp) are off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **A function looked up from a system library on first use** (`DYNAMIC_IMPORT`) keeps it in a `retro_atomic_64_t`: -1 before the lookup, 0 when there is none. It stays constant-initialised.
    - **Shared memory mapped into its own storage** (`shm::map_self`, the memory manager's view of a game's shared memory) keeps the mapping in a `retro_atomic_ptr_t`. When two threads map at once, one installs its mapping with a compare-and-swap; the other takes that one and unmaps its own, as before.
    - **The table of mappings in progress on Windows** keeps each entry's state in a `retro_atomic_int_t`.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` looks up a libc function on first use and checks a missing one reads false. It also checks that two threads mapping one shared memory block into its own storage at once get the same mapping, which is kept and then unmapped. It was shown to fail with the compare-and-swap made a plain store. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to either.


- **The unused fifo_mutex lock and spin_wait spin loop are gone, and asm.hpp's fallback is on `retro_atomic`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** `util/fifo_mutex.hpp` (a lock, which nothing took) and `utils::spin_wait` in `util/asm.hpp` (a loop spinning on a monitor until a condition held, which nothing called) are removed. On a CPU other than x86-64 or ARM64, the store fence on another thread's memory (`utils::memory_barrier`'s portable form) is a `retro_atomic` fetch-and-add of 0 instead of an `atomic_t` one.
  - **Tests:** none: what is removed had no callers, and the fallback is not built on the machines the core targets. A check fails if the lock, the spin loop or that `atomic_t` comes back.


- **YAML values are read in C89 (rpcs3/util/yaml_value.c), in place.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** a YAML scalar read as a boolean, an integer or a double (the settings, the patches, the music and player lists, the PRX validation files) is now read by C89 functions straight from the document's bytes:
    - **Booleans:** they were copied into a lowercased `std::string` first;
    - **Doubles:** they were copied into a `std::string` for `strtod`. The C89 code copies into a stack buffer, or to the heap only for a number longer than 63 bytes;
    - **Integers:** decimal or 0x hex, with a sign, all of the scalar, up to 64 bits, as before. The decimal and hex loops are apart so each base is a constant: no division a digit.
    Measured against the C++ they replace (gcc -O2, x86-64, 100000 values each): integers 7 ns against 9-10 ns, booleans 6-7.5 ns against 10 ns, doubles 58-62 ns against 60-62 ns. They give the same answers on all of them. What reads a node (`util/yaml.hpp`, a handle of a pointer and an index over libretro-common's ryaml) is unchanged.
  - **Not moved:** writing a double with the fewest digits that read back the same stays C++'s `std::to_chars`. A C89 version built on `printf` and `strtod` measured 758 ns against 44 ns, and matching it would take a port of Ryu.
  - **Tests:** `test_yaml_value` (C89) checks every boolean spelling in any case and the refusals, and integers: decimal and hex, the sign, both 64-bit ends, overflow, empty, a sign or 0x alone, other bytes and spaces. It checks doubles with YAML's three spellings each of infinity and NaN and no others, and a number longer than the stack copy, each reading only the bytes it is given. It was shown to fail with the decimal overflow check removed. A check fails if `util/yaml.cpp` goes back to reading values through string copies.


- **lf_queue and lf_bunch (Utilities/lockless.h) are off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **lf_queue** (the many-producer queue a consumer takes whole) keeps its head in one `retro_atomic_128_t`: the item in the low half, and in the high half the word a consumer sleeps on, 1 while the queue is not empty. A push changes both with one 16-byte compare-and-swap, as before.
    - **Cheaper checks:** whether the queue is empty is now one 8-byte load of the item half instead of a 16-byte load.
    - **Sleeping and waking:** a consumer sleeps on the word through the wait engine, and a push into an empty queue wakes it with `retro_waitaddr`. `wait_word()` replaces `get_wait_atomic()`: waiter lists take it, and the SPU compiler's three waits for blocks to compile use it.
    - **lf_bunch** (the list that only grows) keeps its head in a `retro_atomic_ptr_t`, pushed with compare-and-swap loops.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` runs two producers, each on a CPU of its own, pushing 100000 items each while a consumer takes them. It checks none are lost and each producer's come in order, that a consumer asleep on the empty queue is woken by a push, and that two threads pushing to a bunch keep every item. It was shown to fail (it crashed, items lost and the list broken) with a push made a plain store. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the queue or the bunch.


- **Thread control (Utilities/Thread.h and Thread.cpp) is off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **A thread's state word** (its state in the low bits and a notification bit) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`. `notify()` sets the bit and wakes the thread with `retro_waitaddr`, an abort marks it with a compare-and-swap loop and wakes it, and waits sleep on the word through the wait engine, as before. The thread's handle and cycle count are `retro_atomic_64_t`.
    - **A task pushed to a thread** keeps its function in a `retro_atomic_ptr_t` and its done word in a `retro_atomic_int_t`. A waiter sleeps on the done word until the thread has run the task and woken it.
    - **The launch retainer and the core layout** are `retro_atomic_int_t`. The workload splitter's index and count, and the debugger break stamp, are `retro_atomic`.
    - **No spin on macOS:** a new thread spun with `busy_wait` until its creator had stored its handle. It now takes its handle from `pthread_self()`, the same value the creator stores (whichever is first, the other finds the same), as on Linux.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` checks that a thread asleep in `wait_for` is woken by `notify()` long before its 5-second bound, that one asleep in `wait()` is woken by an abort, that a task pushed to a thread runs there and its waiter is woken, and that `map_workload` (from a named thread, as the emulator calls it) hands out every index once. It was shown to fail (the thread slept the whole bound) with `notify()`'s wake removed. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to thread control.


- **Utilities/rXml is gone: trophy configs and the content databases are read with libretro-common's rxml directly.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The wrapper is removed:** `rXml` was a C++ layer over libretro-common's `rxml`. It handed out a new `std::shared_ptr<rXmlNode>` for every node a walk stepped onto, and a `std::string` copy of every name, attribute and text. The trophy code (`TROPUSR`, `sceNpTrophy`, the trophy list overlay) and the content check against the Redump and PSN databases now walk `rxml_node_t` directly (`->children`, `->next`). Small inline helpers in `TROPUSR.h` read names (`xml_name_is`), attributes (`xml_attr`, `""` when missing, as before) and text (`xml_text`) in place, so a walk allocates and copies nothing.
    - **Reading files:** `xml_read_file()` reads a file straight into the buffer the document keeps (`rxml_load_document_owned`), with no copy.
    - **trophy_xml_document** owns its `rxml` document. `First()` is the first node to walk for trophies: `trophyconf`'s first child, or (with another root element) that element alone, as the old walk was.
    - **Text into the guest's structures:** trophy names and details are copied into them with `strlcpy` (libretro-common) instead of `strcpy_trunc`. The structures are zeroed first, as before.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` reads a trophy config written to a file and walks it. It checks the two trophies, the platinum and the hidden one, the ids, a missing attribute reading `""`, and the title with its `&amp;` decoded. It also checks that another root element is walked alone and that a missing file gives no document. It was shown to fail with `First()` walking the root element instead of `trophyconf`'s children. A check fails if `Utilities/rXml` comes back.


- **The atomic bitset (atomic_bs_t: every CPU thread's state, a socket's poll events) is off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** `atomic_bs_t` no longer derives from `atomic_t`. It holds one `retro_atomic_int_t` (the flags of a 32-bit enum), and every operation on it is a `retro_atomic` call:
    - setting and clearing flags (`+=`, `-=`, `test_and_set`, `test_and_reset`) is one atomic or/and, as before;
    - `fetch_op` and `atomic_op` are the same compare-and-swap loops;
    - `store()` is an exchange, a full barrier as the `atomic_t` store was, so code that sets a flag and then reads something else keeps its ordering, and `release()` is a release store;
    - `wait()` sleeps on the word through the wait engine (thread stop checks apply) and `notify_one()`/`notify_all()` wake it.
    The word is the bitset's first member, so the waiter lists that wait on a thread's state wait on the same address as before, and the generated code reading the state at its offset is unchanged.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` checks each operation's old and new flags, that a `fetch_op` that declines stores nothing, and that a waiter is woken by a notify. It also runs two threads, each on a CPU of its own, setting and clearing a flag each 200000 times with none lost. It was shown to fail with `test_and_set` made a load and a store. A check fails if the bitset goes back to `atomic_t`.


- **shared_mutex's word is C89 (Utilities/rw_word.c) on `retro_atomic`, and its slow paths no longer spin.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The word and its paths:** the word (the readers' count, a unit for each writer holding or queued, a hand-over bit and a broken-state bit) is a `retro_atomic_int_t`. Its fast paths (one compare-and-swap or one atomic add) are inline C89 in `rw_word.h`. Its slow paths are C89 in `rw_word.c`, and `mutex.cpp` is gone. A broken state (an underflow or overflow) is still fatal, through `ensure`.
    - **No spinning:** the slow paths tried up to 10 times with `busy_wait` (30 for waiting until free) before sleeping. They now try once and sleep on the word with `retro_waitaddr` until an unlock hands the lock over. Waiting until free without taking the lock is now take-and-give-back, so it never returns while a writer holds the lock.
    - **Removed:** the hardware lock elision variants (`lock_hle` and the rest), which nothing called.
    - **Still a lock:** the four places that take one (the CPU threads' suspend lock, `sys_config` start and stop, the RSX IO map locks and the Vulkan submit lock) are unchanged here. Each needs its own lock-free redesign.
  - **Tests:** `test_rw_word` (C89, and a ThreadSanitizer build) checks readers are counted, that a writer and readers refuse each other, a lone reader's upgrade and downgrade, and a broken word reported by the next lock. It also runs two writers and two readers 100000 rounds each: a reader never sees a writer's two steps half done, the count ends right and the word ends at 0. It was shown to fail with the wait returning without being handed the lock. A check fails if `mutex.cpp`, `atomic_t` or a spin comes back.


- **The settings' values (Utilities/Config.h) are off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** each setting's value can be changed by the frontend while the emulator threads read it, so it is atomic. A setting holds its value in a `retro_atomic_int_t` (on/off), a `retro_atomic_64_t` (a choice, a whole number of either sign, a decimal as its bits) or a `retro_atomic_128_t` (128-bit). Every read and write goes through the setting's own `get()` and `set()` as before, now a plain acquire load and a release store, so the code reading settings is unchanged and compiles to the same loads.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` checks that a setting of each kind keeps its default, takes a new value (the ends of its range among them), reads and writes its text, refuses text out of range and goes back to its default. It also checks that a 128-bit setting switched between two values by one thread is never seen half of each by another (200000 reads, each thread on a CPU of its own). It was shown to fail with the 128-bit value stored and read in two halves. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the settings' values.


- **The JIT's memory allocators are C89 (Utilities/jit_bump.c) on `retro_atomic`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **Code and data ranges:** the JIT's code and data each come from 1 GiB of reserved memory, handed out by moving a counter on. The counter's low half is the next free byte and its high half is how far the range is committed. It is a `retro_atomic_64_t`, taken with the compare-and-swap loop `atomic_op` ran, now in C89: `jit_bump_alloc()`. An allocation past the end fails, and every one after it too, as before. `jit_bump_commit()` raises the committed top once more memory is committed.
    - **The 16 MiB runtime for asmjit's own needs:** its bump pointer is a `retro_atomic_ptr_t`, taken by `jit_bump_ptr_alloc()`.
    - **The ASMJIT dump's index and data counters** (written only when the ASMJIT folder exists) are `retro_atomic_64_t`. An entry's address is stored last with a release store, so a reader of the dump sees it complete.
  - **Tests:** `test_jit_bump` (C89, and a ThreadSanitizer build) checks offsets come aligned and in order, the committed top steps up and stays, a failure past the limit sticks, and the pointer form does the same within its range. It also runs two threads, each on a CPU of its own, taking a block each at once 200000 times, and checks they never take the same one. It was shown to fail (12731 rounds where both took the same block) with the compare-and-swap made a plain store. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the JIT allocators.


- **The file layer's Windows position and the directory size cancel flag are off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** a Windows file's position (read and written by overlapped I/O, so two threads can share a file) is a `retro_atomic_64_t`, moved on with a fetch-and-add as before. `fs::get_dir_size()`'s optional cancel flag is a `retro_atomic_int_t` pointer; no caller passes one.
  - **Tests:** none at runtime: the position is in the Windows file code, which the Linux test machine does not run. It is compile-checked with MinGW. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the file layer.


- **"Preferred SPU Threads": the instruction slot counts are C89 (Emu/Cell/spu_pc_table.c), and the SPU waits for a slot instead of sleeping in a loop.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The counts:** with Preferred SPU Threads set, an SPU about to run an MFC command counts itself in at its instruction's slot, and waits while that slot already has as many SPUs as the setting allows. The 65536 counts were an `std::array` of `atomic_t<u8>`. They are now C89, a byte each, four to a `retro_atomic_int_t` word, so the table is the same 64 KiB. Entering is one atomic add, and leaving is one atomic subtract when nobody waits.
    - **The wait:** it slept 1 ms at a time and read the host's clock until the SPU delay penalty ran out. A waiter now sets the top bit of its slot's byte and sleeps on the word with `retro_waitaddr`, bounded by the delay penalty. The SPU leaving the slot sees the bit, clears it and wakes the waiters, so a waiter goes on as soon as the slot has room instead of on the next 1 ms tick.
    - **No penalty:** with a delay penalty of 0, the SPU spun for the slot's count times 100 cycles before going on anyway. It now goes on at once.
    - **The GETLLAR wait statistics** (trace log only) are `retro_atomic_int_t`.
  - **Tests:** `test_spu_pc_table` (C89, and a ThreadSanitizer build) checks the counts of a word's four slots are kept apart, that a wait below the limit returns at once and one nobody ends runs out its bound, that a waiter on a full slot is woken by the leave long before its 2-second bound, and that four threads entering and leaving one word's slots 200000 times each lose no count and leave a neighbour's alone. It was shown to fail with the leave's wake removed. A check fails if the table goes back to `atomic_t` or the sleep loop comes back.


- **The SPU recompiler's compiled blocks, dispatcher and counters are off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **A compiled block (spu_item):** the function, the block's ubertrampoline, and the flags for written to the SPU cache and logged are `retro_atomic_ptr_t` and `retro_atomic_int_t`. `install_compiled()` puts a function in only where none is (a compare-and-swap), and `set_compiled()` replaces one from the fast compiler, as before. A thread that finds another compiling the same block waits in `wait_compiled()` on the ready word through the wait engine, and `notify_compiled()` wakes it. `install_trampoline()` gives back the trampoline already there, as `compare_and_swap` did. `take_once()` and `give_back()` handle the two flags.
    - **The dispatcher:** the 2^20 entries the generated code jumps through are `retro_atomic_ptr_t`, 8 bytes each as before, so the generated code is unchanged. An ubertrampoline is put in with a compare-and-swap loop that keeps a newer one.
    - **The cache build's counters** (next function, next precompile entry, failure) and the pattern statistics the compiler threads count for the log are `retro_atomic`.
    - **The block sampler's counts** are plain integers: only the thread that picks the next block to compile reads and writes them (`idm::select` runs its callback there).
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` checks that a block's function goes in only where none is (and is replaced when asked), that a trampoline is the same, and that the cache flag is taken once. It also checks that a thread waiting on another's compile is woken once the function is in. It was shown to fail with the function put in over one already there, and to hang with the wake removed. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the recompiler's blocks.


- **The reservation waiter slots are off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** an SPU waiting for a reservation's cache line to change counts itself into one of 1024 slots, chosen by address and time, and sleeps on the slot's wait flag until a store to the line notifies it, or until its bound (50-200 µs, as before). Each slot is one `retro_atomic_64_t` on a 128-byte line of its own: the flag in the low half and the count of waiters in the high half. `vm::reservation_waiter_op()` changes both with one compare-and-swap loop, as `atomic_op` and `fetch_op` did. `reservation_notifier_wait()` sleeps on the flag through the wait engine, so the thread stop checks and the SPU's one-time callback still apply. `reservation_notifier_wake()` wakes it, and a postponed notify hands lv2 the flag's address to wake later, as before.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` checks that a waiter makes the flag odd and is counted, that a notify makes the flag even once (a second finds nothing to wake), and that a thread asleep on the flag is woken by the notify long before its 3-second bound. It was shown to fail (the thread slept the whole 3 seconds) with the notify's wake removed. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the slots.


- **The reservation word is off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The word:** each 128-byte cache line of guest memory has a reservation word: the time of its last update and its lock bits, used by the SPU and PPU atomics (GETLLAR/PUTLLC, lwarx/stwcx), cellSpurs and the RSX FIFO's reads. It is a `retro_atomic_64_t` in reservation memory, as before. `vm::reservation_acquire()` is gone. `vm::reservation_word()` gives the word, and `reservation_time()` reads it. `reservation_load()`, `reservation_cas()`, `reservation_add()` (the old value, as `fetch_add`/`fetch_sub` gave), `reservation_release()` and `reservation_fetch_op()` (a compare-and-swap loop, as `fetch_op` did) work on it.
    - **The 16-byte views:** the word with the shared memory base after it (a full-line store from another SPU) and the byte-lock bits after that (a partial store) are `retro_atomic_128_t`, reached by `vm::reservation_wide()` and `reservation_byte_bits()`. They are changed with a 16-byte compare-and-swap loop.
    - **One difference:** the SPU's MONITORX/MWAITX wait (AMD) took the reservation word's value before the monitor was armed, so a change between the two could be missed until the wait timed out. It now passes the word itself, read after the monitor is armed, as `atomic_t`'s reference did.
    - **Wakes:** the notify on the word after a light atomic op is a `retro_waitaddr` notify on its low half.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` checks where the word and its two views sit in reservation memory. It also checks that two threads, each on a CPU of its own, step one word's time on at once 200000 times each with none lost, and the lock bit refusing a shared lock and a second lock. It was shown to fail with `reservation_fetch_op`'s compare-and-swap made a plain store. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the reservation word.


- **A memory block's id (vm::block_t) is off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** each memory block (a location such as main memory, or one a game maps) takes a unique id from a counter, and the id is cleared to 0 once, on unmapping, so only one unmap frees the block's memory. The id and the counter are `retro_atomic_64_t`. The id is taken with a fetch-and-add and cleared with an exchange, as before.
  - **Tests:** none at runtime: blocks are made only with the memory manager set up for a game. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the id.


- **The passive lock slots (vm::g_locks) are off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** each PPU thread holds one slot while it reads guest memory without the range locks. The slot holds the thread, or null. The slots and the thread's own slot pointer are `retro_atomic_ptr_t`. `vm::passive_lock_owner()` reads a slot, and `vm::passive_lock_drop()` gives it up, but only for the thread holding it, with a compare-and-swap as before. A thread taking a slot is followed by a full barrier before it reads the writers' bits (free on x86, where the compare-and-swap is one). A writer setting its bit has the same barrier before it reads the slots, so one of them always sees the other. The memory manager, the PPU thread and the CPU thread use them.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` checks that a slot's owner is read back and that only the thread holding the slot gives it up. It was shown to fail with the slot given up whoever asks. The barrier pairing is the one `test_vm_range_locks` already checks. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the slots.


- **The memory range locks are off `atomic_t`, and their scans are C89 (Emu/Memory/vm_range_locks.c).** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **The slots:** the 64 range lock slots (the range a thread is reading or writing guest memory over, address in the low half, size and flags in the high) and the two words of slot bits (the slots in use, the slots locked for writing) are `retro_atomic_64_t`s, each on a 128-byte line of its own as before. `vm::range_lock_t` is the slot; `range_lock_value()`, `range_lock_publish()`, `range_lock_release()`, `range_lock_writers()` and `range_lock_main()` read and set them. The SPU thread, lv2's reservation wait, PPU thread exit and the CPU thread use them.
    - **Ordering:** a slot set before the writers' bits are looked at, and a writer's bit set before the slots are, keep the full barrier `atomic_t`'s store and `bit_test_set` gave: on x86 an exchange (one `xchg`, as before), elsewhere a release store and a full fence.
    - **The C module:** taking a slot (a compare-and-swap loop, the top bit kept for memory setting changes and the one below it never handed out, as before), giving one back, and the three scans (a reader's look for writers over its range, a memory setting change's wait for readers, a writer's wait for readers of one reservation) are C89 in `vm_range_locks.c`, built in `Makefile.common`. The scans gather the slots that hit instead of clearing those that miss, and keep the common case (a slot not in use) on the straight path. Measured against the C++ they replace (gcc -O2, x86-64, 32 and 64 slots, 20 million calls): the reader scan 20-26 ns against 25-28 ns, the writer scan 24-28 ns against 27-41 ns, with the same answers. The memory setting change's scan was not measured.
    - **One difference:** a writer's scan over a range crossing the top of the 32-bit space read one entry past the end of the shared memory table; the page now wraps to 0 as the address does.
  - **Tests:** `test_vm_range_locks` (C89, and a ThreadSanitizer build): slots taken lowest first and given back; the three scans against the C++ code written out again, over hand-picked cases and 200000 random sets of slots; four threads taking and giving back slots at once with none held twice; and a reader setting its slot while a writer sets its bit, on two CPUs, 200000 rounds, where at least one must see the other. It was shown to fail with the reader's end not taken through the mirror, with the slot's exchange made a plain release store (895 to 1429 rounds where neither saw the other), and (under AddressSanitizer) with the page not wrapped. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the range locks.


- **The shared memory mirror table (vm::g_shmem) is off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** the 65536 entries, one per 64K page (the shared memory mirror's base, or 0), are `retro_atomic_64_t`s. `vm::shmem_of()` reads one. The memory manager sets them as shared memory is mapped and unmapped, and the range locks and the SPU's reservation code read them. They are zeroed one by one at the start, where a `memset` was.
  - **Tests:** none at runtime: they are set as a game maps shared memory. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the table.


- **The SPU cache line waiters, the raw SPU MMIO log entry and the reservation checker flag are off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:**
    - **Cache line waiters:** the six waiters SPUs register on a reservation's cache line (its hash, address and count of waiters) are `retro_atomic_64_t`s, each on a 128-byte line of its own as before. `waiter_load()` reads one, and `waiter_op()` changes one with a compare-and-swap loop, as `fetch_op` and `atomic_op` did. The SPU thread and lv2's reservation notifier use them.
    - **MMIO log entry:** the last raw SPU MMIO log entry (offset, value, time, used only with trace logging) is a `retro_atomic_128_t`. It is changed with a 16-byte compare-and-swap and starts at all ones, as its defaults did.
    - **Reservation checker flag:** the flag that picks the one SPU that checks reservation data is a `retro_atomic_int_t`.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` checks a waiter's word is a cache line of its own, and that two threads, each on a CPU of its own, change one waiter at once 200000 times each with none lost. It was shown to fail with `waiter_op`'s compare-and-swap made a plain store. The MMIO log entry and the flag have no runtime test, because they need a game's SPUs. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to any of them.


- **An SPU's status and NPC word is off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** the status register and the next program counter are one `retro_atomic_64_t`, held to 8 bytes by a static assert. `load_status_npc()` reads them whole and `store_status_npc()` sets them. `status_npc_op()` changes them with a compare-and-swap loop, as `atomic_op` and `fetch_op` did. Setting the NPC alone, and the status alone on a savestate's load, were stores into half the word; they are now compare-and-swaps on the whole word. The SPU thread, the raw SPU MMIO and sys_spu use them.
  - **Savestates:** the status is written and read as the `u32` it was, and the NPC is set from the program counter on load as before, so the format is unchanged.
  - **Tests:** none at runtime: the word belongs to an SPU thread, which needs a game's SPU. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to it.


- **An SPU's event word is off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** the events, their mask, the lock count and the waiting and count bits are one `retro_atomic_64_t`. It stays 8 bytes at its own offset, where both recompilers load it, held by a static assert on the union's size. `load_events()` reads it whole. `events_op()` and `events_fetch_op()` change it with a compare-and-swap loop, giving back what `atomic_op` and `fetch_op` gave. The SPU thread and both recompilers' helpers use them.
  - **Savestates:** the word is written and read as the `u64` it was, so the format is unchanged.
  - **Tests:** none at runtime: the word belongs to an SPU thread, which needs a game's SPU and its local store mapped. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to it.


- **An SPU's interrupt control is off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** each interrupt class's mask and status are `retro_atomic_64_t`s. Status bits are raised with `retro_atomic_fetch_or_64`, and a new one still tells the handler's thread, as before. They are cleared with `retro_atomic_fetch_and_64`. sys_spu's raw SPU calls and sys_interrupt's handler thread read and set them through `get_mask()`, `get_stat()` and the `retro_atomic` calls.
  - **Savestates:** the mask and status are written and read as the `u64`s they were, so the format is unchanged.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` checks that only the mask's bits are set and that they clear. Two threads, each on a CPU of its own, set different bits at once 20000 times, and both must be kept. It was shown to fail with the fetch-or made a load and a store. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the interrupt control.


- **The SPU's inbound mailbox is off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** the mailbox's state (waiting byte, count, and its first three values) is one `retro_atomic_128_t`. It stays 16 bytes at the mailbox's own address, where the ASMJIT recompiler loads it whole and the LLVM recompiler reads its first word, held by static asserts. `values_op()` and `values_fetch_op()` change it with a 16-byte compare-and-swap, as `atomic_op` and `fetch_op` did. Taking the waiting byte, and setting the first value from a syscall's answer, were one-byte and four-byte stores into the 16-byte word; they are now compare-and-swaps on the whole word. The count is read from the low half alone. The fourth value is a `retro_atomic_int_t` and the pending value a `retro_atomic_64_t`. A waiting SPU waits on the first word, as before.
  - **Savestates:** the mailbox is saved as its count and values, read through `try_read` and set with `set_values`, so the format is unchanged.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` pushes five values and checks four in order, the fifth replacing the last. It checks that a value is set only when the mailbox is empty, and that two threads pushing at once (each on a CPU of its own) are both counted, 20000 times. It also checks that a marked waiter is woken by a push, 300 times. It was shown to fail with the 16-byte compare-and-swap made a plain store, and with the push's wake taken out. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to the mailbox.


- **An SPU channel is off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** a channel's word (value, wait, occupy and count bits) and its pending value are `retro_atomic_64_t`s. The word stays at offset 0 and 8 bytes, where the LLVM recompiler's code reads and writes it, held by a static assert. `fetch_data()` changes it with a compare-and-swap loop and gives back what `fetch_op` gave. The bit clears are `retro_atomic_fetch_and_64`. A waiting SPU waits on the word's upper half, through `set_raw` and `thread_ctrl::wait_on_custom` as before. Pushes wake it with `retro_waitaddr`.
  - **Savestates:** the eight channels saved (tag, stall and atomic status, both outbound mailboxes, both signal notifications, and the exit status) are written and read through the `u64`s they were, so the format is unchanged.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` pushes, reads and pops a channel. Two threads push into an empty channel at once, 20000 times, and one of them must land. A waiter on the upper half, marked as `pop_wait` marks it, must be woken by a push, 300 times. It was shown to fail with the push's wake taken out, and with `fetch_data`'s compare-and-swap made a plain store. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to `spu_channel`.


- **An SPU thread's proxy mask, run control, stop count and exit status are off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** the MFC proxy query mask, the run control register, the raw SPU stop count and the exit status kept until the group ends are `retro_atomic_int_t`s. A stop request on a raw SPU waits on the stop count with `retro_waitaddr`, as it did on the `atomic_t`. The breakpoint flag is one too. The breakpoint list is plain bytes, since nothing in the core sets a breakpoint, so the list stays empty.
  - **Savestates:** the proxy mask and run control are written and read through the `u32`s they were, so the format is unchanged.
  - **Tests:** none at runtime: they are set and read by a game's SPU and raw SPU MMIO. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to them.


- **The raw SPU counters and the SPU compile work count are off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** the count of raw SPUs, the five raw SPU id slots and the count of SPU programs being compiled are `retro_atomic_int_t`s. A raw SPU's place and its id slot are taken with `spu_thread::try_inc_below()`, a compare-and-swap loop that raises the word only below a limit, as `try_inc` did. sys_spu, RawSPUThread, the SPU thread and the SPU recompiler use them. Nothing saved in savestates changes.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` checks that `try_inc_below()` stops at its limit, and that of two threads taking the last place at once one gets it, 20000 times. It was shown to fail with the compare-and-swap made a plain store. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to these counters.


- **The lock-free array and FIFO (Utilities/lockless.h) are off `atomic_t`.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** `lf_array`'s link to its next block is a `retro_atomic_ptr_t`, installed once with a compare-and-swap; a thread that loses goes on to the block that won, as before. `lf_fifo`'s push and pop counter is a `retro_atomic_64_t`. A place is taken with a fetch-add, and letting places go (which empties the counter to 0 when the last is let go) is a compare-and-swap loop, where `atomic_op` was. These carry a PPU thread's command queue, the shader cache's work lists, the progress dialog's text queue and the native memory map list. `lf_queue`'s head and its wait word are left for the thread wait work, since `thread_ctrl::wait_on` waits on them.
  - **Tests:** `test_emu_waits` has two threads grow one array at once and checks they see the same element at each index, 2000 times. It also checks that a FIFO's places, taken by two threads at once, are each taken once, and that it empties to 0. It was shown to fail with the link's compare-and-swap made a plain store, with the emptying taken out, and with the fetch-add made a load and a store. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to either.


- **A PPU thread's command queue is off `atomic_t`, the last of PPUThread.h.** Not yet run on a game.
  - **What changed:** each of the queue's 127 slots is a `retro_atomic_64_t` holding a `cmd64`'s word. A command's tail is written relaxed and its head with a release store, as before. The thread takes the head with an exchange to zero and reads the tail with acquire loads. The queue's own counters (lf_fifo, in Utilities/lockless.h) are not changed here.
  - **Tests:** none at runtime: commands are pushed to a game's PPU threads. A check fails if `atomic_t` comes back to PPUThread.h.


- **The sysutil callback word's race test now races.** A test change only.
  - **What changed:** it made two new threads for each of 500 rounds. The first was mostly done before the second started, so an install that was a plain store instead of a compare-and-swap still passed.
